OIL FUTURES: Crude slides after IEA offers support for G7 diesel release
IEA backs faster release of diesel stocks
Hormuz shipping falls amid supply fears
US crude stocks hit three-week low
Crude oil futures settled lower Oct. 7 after the International Energy Agency offered support for the emergency release of European diesel stocks.
NYMEX November WTI settled $1.16/barrel lower at $88.28/b and ICE December Brent declined 38 cents/b to $100.20/b.
After a meeting of its member governments on Oct. 7, the IEA said it backed the faster release of stocks already agreed upon in March, when the organization committed to draw an unprecedented 400 million barrels of crude and oil products from its collective reserves. Members also supported the prioritized release of diesel "to the extent possible, given the tightness in diesel markets," the IEA said in a statement.
The IEA announcement follows an Oct. 2 statement from G7 leaders committing to release 100 million barrels of oil via the agency, after pressure from the US to take stronger measures to cool diesel prices. According to the IEA, 100 million barrels of oil represents the volume of stocks committed in March but not yet released, although some countries have overfilled their targets.
NYMEX November RBOB declined 3.90 cents/gallon to $3.2342/gal and November ULSD settled 5.33 cents/gal higher at $4.6227/gal.

International Crude
Dated Brent differential buoyed by short-haul demand despite high freight
Differential rises over $3/b across five sessions, approaches record high
Short haul North Sea grades hold steady as adjacent markets struggle
Longer balances expected for November, but uncertainty remains
The Dated Brent physical differential has seen a bullish trajectory across October to date, with prompt North Sea values buoyed by demand for short-haul grades as adjacent markets struggled with high freight rates.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the Dated Brent physical differential at an $18.92/b premium over the North Sea Dated strip Oct. 7, up $3.91/b across the previous five pricing sessions.
The differential is now 33.5 cents/b away from its all-time high of a $19.255/b premium reached April 13.
The Dated Brent differential reflects the most competitive differential among Brent, Forties, Oseberg, Ekofisk, Troll and WTI Midland grades each day during the 10-day to one-month forward assessment period.
As freight rates between Europe and key supply regions continue to reach record high values, market participants continued to note interest for October-loading North Sea cargoes on comparatively lower cross-UK Continent freight rates for shorter journeys.
"The North Sea [market] is unique because of its short haul nature, while the long-hauls are suffering," said a Europe-based North Sea crude trader.
"Freight is so high it breaks a lot of logistical logic," said a second trader, adding that some refiners had turned to shorter haul North Sea grades "for security of supply".
The Oct. 5-7 sessions of the Platts Market on Close assessment process had seen interest demonstrated for all six Dated Brent basket grades on both an FOB and CIF Rotterdam basis. Notably, bids for Oseberg FOB cargoes were left outstanding at premiums as high as $22.45/b over Dated Brent, while bids for WTI Midland CIF Rotterdam cargoes were shown as high as $22.65/b.

grades, including Brent Blend and Forties, above the $20/b mark during Oct. 7 European afternoon trading.
While prompt barrels have seen support, supply balances for November are expected to improve across both the sweet and sour segments, with producers across the Americas prioritizing Europe as an export destination to save on longer-haul freight costs to Asia.
As such, with less volumes headed East, market participants are anticipating more volumes of WTI Midland and Latin American crudes -- which traditionally compete with Johan Sverdrup -- headed to Europe, among others.
"For sure Midland is coming, the levels will depend on freight as everything does these days but shorter voyages would be prioritized," said a third trader.
Reflecting both the tightness on the prompt and weaker prospects further out, the backwardated structure across the Brent CFD strip widened further to its steepest value since midApril at the Oct. 7 London close.
The Brent CFD settling against week 2, currently Oct. 12-16, was assessed at a $17.54/b premium over its week 6 counterpart, having widened $3.69/b over the previous five pricing sessions.
Regardless, the volatility surrounding freight economics and ongoing supply developments, including various oil stock releases, has kept sentiment deeply uncertain as to supply balances going forward.
"The curves still imply that differentials come off sharply after this week," said a fourth trader, referring to the steeply backwardated structure between the Brent CFDs settling across prompt weeks.
However, "we're not sure how sharp [the correction] will be with current market conditions, the uncertainty is just too big," the trader added.
-- Natasha Tan
GME Oman crude futures Sept traded volumes decline 2.65% on month
Oman cash prices jump to $113.77/b
Open interest drops 11.77% to 15,252 lots
Middle East supply volatility hits region
The total traded volume of GME Oman crude oil futures in September fell 2.65% month over month to 173,033 lots, Gulf Mercantile Exchange data showed Oct. 7.
All contracts traded on the GME were Oman crude futures. The GME also lists other crude futures contracts, including Oman crude financial futures and the Brent-Oman futures spread.
Open interest for the GME Oman crude futures contract stood at 15,252 lots Sept. 30, down 11.77% from the end of August when open interest closed the month at 17,286 lots.
GME Oman futures is a physically delivered futures contract for the Oman crude blend.
Platts cash Oman averaged $113.77/barrel in September, up

from $88.75/b in August. Its spread against same-month Dubai futures averaged a premium of $21.65/b in September, up from $9.59/b in August.
Middle East crude supply was volatile in September as the shutdown of Saudi Arabia's East-West pipeline, as well as reduced Iranian crude exports, sent regional cash differentials to multi-month highs, though prices cooled toward the end of September as producers and traders ramped up spot offers.
Spot trade in October is expected to be thinned by China's Golden Week holiday over Oct. 1-7, though market participants will be keenly watching for vessel transits through the Strait of Hormuz amid an uptick in voyages through the channel in September.

Sep IFAD Murban traded volume falls 89% MOM to 7,066 lots
Traded volume at lowest level since launch Open interest drops 87% as contracts wind down

IFAD Murban crude futures traded volume fell 89.2% month over month to 7,066 lots in September, marking the lowest level since trading began March 29, 2021, according to data from the Intercontinental Exchange's ICE Futures Abu Dhabi market on Oct. 7.
IFAD is the UAE-based arm of Intercontinental Exchange. Traded volume for front-month November Murban first-line futures declined to 4,277 lots in September from 53,107 lots for front-month October Murban first-line futures in August, the data showed. The front-month October Murban first-line futures contract concluded trading Aug. 28, one day earlier than usual because of the UK bank holiday on Aug. 31. One futures lot is equivalent to 1,000 barrels. Crude oil traders attributed the drop in trading volumes to Abu Dhabi National Oil Co.'s July 31 announcement that it would replace its IFAD-linked Murban futures pricing mechanism with a prompt-month Platts Dubai benchmark for crude official selling prices, effective Nov. 1, aligning pricing more closely with cargo loading schedules. IFAD said in July it would suspend Murban futures and related cash-settled contract months that had no open interest as of July 31, while contracts with existing positions would continue trading until expiry or until open interest falls to zero. Open interest for front-month November first-line futures declined to 2,390 lots on Sept. 30 from 25,772 lots at the close of trading for the front-month October first-line futures contract on Aug. 28, the data showed.
No exchange of futures for physical volumes were recorded, compared with 4,095 lots in August, according to the data.
Middle East crude supply was volatile in September, with regional cash differentials hitting multimonth highs, though prices cooled toward the end of the month as producers and traders ramped up spot offers.
Traders said most November-loading Murban crude cargoes had traded in September at differentials to the grade's OSP, with premiums of 20-50 cents/b to the OSP on a FOB basis.
Platts Cash Murban averaged $114.51/b in September, up from $91.36/b in August. The spread to same-month Dubai futures averaged a $22.39/b premium in September, up from $12.20/b in August, Platts data showed.

Bahrain transport minister sees potential in moving Gulf crude by rail
GCC ministers will meet to finalize rail rules
Pipelines currently bypass Hormuz
Transporting crude by rail within Gulf Cooperation Council countries remains a "potential," with the focus currently on pipelines to ship crude oil to avoid the Strait of Hormuz, the transportation and telecommunications minister of Bahrain told Platts in Fujairah, UAE, on Oct. 7.
GCC transportation ministers are set to meet in Bahrain in early November to agree on the regulations for a rail network connecting all six member countries, Abdullah Bin Ahmed Al Khalifa, Bahrain's minister of transportation and telecommunications, said on the sidelines of the Energy Markets Forum.
The GCC is made up of Saudi Arabia, the UAE, Bahrain, Oman, Kuwait and Qatar. The minister said Iraq could join as well.
Adding crude to the list of commodities that can be transported via railroad is a "potential," he said.

Crude oil claims greater share of Fujairah port throughput during war: official
Crude share rises to 40% from 10% at port
Ship fuel demand recovers from record low
Exports surpass 6 million b/d in September
The Port of Fujairah's throughput has climbed to about 40% crude oil trading, with the remainder refined products, Martijn Heijboer, senior manager of business development at the Fujairah Ports Authority, said at a conference in Fujairah Oct. 7.
Crude oil's share was 10% before the Middle East war, Heijboer told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, on the sidelines of the Energy Markets Forum.
Ship fuel demand is "in recovery mode" after falling to record lows. In July, sales rose to 234,175 cubic meters from 88,659 cubic meters in June, the all-time low, he said.
The Fujairah Ports Authority comprises the Port of Fujairah, Dibba Port for dry bulk, and Rughaylat Port, which is under construction and scheduled for completion in 2028. It will be operated by DP World under a 50-year concession agreement.
Crude oil exports from Fujairah climbed to more than 2 million barrels/day for the first time in at least a decade as Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. rerouted oil to bypass the Strait of Hormuz, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data. It was up to 6.2 million b/d by September, the CAS data showed.
-- Claudia Carpenter, Lauren Holtmeier
Gulf oil exports could recover to preconflict levels by mid-2027: World Bank
Inventory depletion threatens demand destruction
Governments deploy subsidies as primary response
Export curbs could compound impact of high prices
Oil exports from the Gulf region could return to preconflict levels by mid-2027, but a prolonged disruption to shipments could further strain already depleted inventories and lift prices to levels that may threaten demand, the World Bank said in its latest East Asia and Pacific Economic Update, released Oct. 6.
"In the most likely, baseline scenario, oil exports from the Gulf region mostly return to preconflict levels by mid-2027. Should shipments remain blocked for longer, dwindling inventories will require prices to rise high enough to reduce demand by an amount equivalent to the supply shock. As inventories approach critical operational levels, physical scarcity could cause prices to spike," the report said.

As the Strait of Hormuz handles roughly 20% of global petroleum consumption and 20% of global LNG trade, disruptions have lifted crude oil prices to levels not seen since the Russian Federation's initial invasion of Ukraine in 2022, the report said. Prices for many refined energy products, such as diesel and jet fuel, have increased even more, according to the report.
Crude prices finished a volatile session on Oct. 6 as markets weighed improved Middle East energy flows against persistent security threats. NYMEX November WTI settled 1 cent/b higher at $89.44/b, while ICE December Brent increased 26 cents/b to $100.58/b.
The report said countries adopted various measures to compensate for lost oil supplies. Inventories have been drawn down, with stockpiles falling below historical norms in many cases. Some countries, notably China, have reduced oil imports by switching to alternative energy sources or drawing on inventories, the report said.
Various measures In general, governments have relied on four broad types
of measures to cope with the energy-price shock: consumer subsidies and price support, such as capping or freezing fuel and electricity prices and cutting fuel taxes; demand-side measures that curb consumption directly, such as fuel rationing, driving restrictions and work-from-home or shortened-week mandates; social protection for vulnerable households, mainly cash transfers and in-kind distribution of fuel or essentials; and supply-security measures, such as releasing strategic reserves and imposing trade controls, according to the report.
"Subsidies have been by far the most common policy response among emerging and developing economies. Many countries have also ramped up subsidies that were already in place. Countries with substantial subsidies in place generally have had smaller increases in retail gasoline prices than nonsubsidizers," the report said.
Governments have drawn down oil and natural gas stocks that were unusually full before the conflict in the Middle East and are now substantially depleted, the report said. Trade networks for crude oil and refined goods have been reconfigured, and some ships have continued to transit despite disruptions in the Strait of Hormuz, according to the report.
Ship traffic through the Strait of Hormuz fell to 22 crossings on Oct. 5 from 26 on Oct. 4, as two incidents occurred around the chokepoint that day, S&P Global Commodities at Sea said in a report Oct. 6.
Seven energy tankers made inbound transits Oct. 5, down from 15 the previous day, CAS said. The remaining crossings included six bulk carriers, one container ship, and eight vessels from other segments, according to CAS. Of the total crossings, 14 were outbound and eight were inbound, CAS said, adding that 12 compliant vessels were identified as having crossed, eight of them without active AIS signals.
"Previous spikes in oil prices were not foreseen by markets, and new ones could be triggered in a variety of ways. Other oil-

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

5

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 7, 2026

supplying regions could rein in recent output expansion, including due to conflict. The drawdown of oil inventories may need to be curtailed as reserves dwindle," the World Bank report said.
"Fiscal support may prove unsustainable given stretched fiscal positions in many countries. A renewed rise in energy prices may therefore be more damaging to the global economy than observed during the initial increase," the report added.
Export restrictions Export restrictions could compound the impact of a renewed
rise in fossil-fuel prices, the report said. During the current crisis, countries that produce or refine energy products have mostly allowed markets to balance supply and demand, with the notable exception of Russia, according to the report.
"In previous episodes of global supply disruption, however, countries have frequently resorted to export restrictions to avoid domestic shortages. These weaken market incentives and amplify scarcity during a crisis, and would amplify any increase in energy prices," the report said.
Shortages of oil, fertilizers and aluminum would weigh on the global economy: oil shortages could raise transport and logistics costs; fertilizer shortages would threaten agricultural production and food prices; and aluminum shortages would disrupt construction, transport equipment, manufacturing and renewable-energy supply chains, according to the report.
"Given that many refined energy products, such as jet fuel and diesel, have already increased far more than crude oil, further increases could weigh heavily on countries reliant on airborne travel or those with limited domestic refining capacity, such as Pacific Island Countries," the report said.
Further increases in energy prices could lead to supply-chain disruptions, higher inflation and tighter financial conditions as major central banks raise policy rates, according to the report. This could also trigger a deterioration in risk appetite in financial markets, the report said.
"The impact of such a scenario of persistently higher energy prices on individual countries depends on two factors -- their exposure to global energy markets and their use of policies to dampen shocks," the report said.
-- Sambit Mohanty

Repsol Q3 upstream output rises, refining margin breaks record
Q3 production hits 574,000 boe/d, up 4%
Downstream margin reaches record $36.20/b
North American output surges 21% YOY
Spanish integrated energy group Repsol SA increased its upstream oil and gas production by 4% year over year to 574,000 barrels of oil equivalent/day in the third quarter, the company said in a stock market filing Oct. 6.
Repsol's downstream margin for the period reached a record high, based on company data.
Upstream output, which reached the largest quarterly total since Q2 2024, was driven by a 21% year year-over-year increase in North American output to 219,000 boe/d, Repsol said, without providing a breakdown. The figures are all provisional, with final volumes to be published Oct. 29.
Repsol and partner Santos in May announced the start-up of production from the Alaskan North Slope Pikka field, which had a production target of 40,000 boe/d during H2 2026.
The Madrid-based company also has producing assets in Pennsylvania, Texas and the Gulf of Mexico.
In its other regions, Latin American net production, which includes Venezuela, was 237,000 boe/d, down 6% year over year, Repsol said, without providing a breakdown.
Europe, Africa and "rest of world" output dropped 33% year over year to 118,000 boe/d, amid disruption to output from Libya's El Sharara field.

On Sept. 22, Libya's National Oil Corp. issued a third force majeure warning since August after the pipeline and the gates of the Zawiya refinery and Libyan state fuel marketer were shut by armed groups. The incidents affected flows from the field.
For the first nine months of 2026, Repsol's average production was about 557,000 boe/d, short of a company forecast of between 560,000 boe/d to 570,000 boe/d for 2026 that it published in July.
Record margin Repsol's Q3 2026 Spanish downstream refining margin,
based on regional benchmarks, increased 159% year over year to $36.20/b, the company said in its filing. This beat the previous record high of $23.20/b from Q2 2022, company data showed.
The Q3 indicator was greater than Repsol's budgeted $6.50/b benchmark margin for the 2026 to 2028 period.
For the first nine months, the indicator has averaged roughly $20.37/b, according to company data.

Repsol did not respond to a request for comment Oct. 6. Repsol owns and manages five refining complexes in Spain -- Cartagena, A Coruna, Bilbao, Puertollano and Tarragona -- with a total capacity of around 896,000 b/d, according to company information. The overall distillation rate in Q3 fell 5% year over year to 81.3%, the company reported, while the conversion rate was down 4% year over year to 97.6%. A topping unit at Cartagena that suffered a fire in January was due to remain offline until around the end of 2026, Repsol CEO Imaz said July 23, while Bilbao reported some work during Q3, and Cartagena was due to carry out a change of catalyst at the hydrocracker unit in September, meaning a few days' halt, the company said in July. Looking ahead, Puertollano could carry out a turnaround of its conversion units in Q4, with no time frame supplied for the work, Repsol said in July. Downstream, Repsol's petrochemical margin indicator was 387/mt, up 50% year over year and above the 2025 full-year average of 252/mt. Repsol operates 4.8 million mt/year of base and derivative petrochemical capacity in Spain and Portugal.

ASEAN net oil product imports to reach 2.2 million b/d by 2031: IEA
Regional demand grows 130,000 b/d yearly
Upstream output drops to 1.6 million b/d
LPG imports double, creating vulnerability
Southeast Asia's net oil product imports are projected to reach 2.2 million barrels/day by 2031 as rising demand outpaces

domestic crude production and the capabilities of the region's aging refineries, the International Energy Agency said.
Regional crude import requirements are expected to approach 3 million b/d by the same year, leaving ASEAN increasingly exposed to supply disruptions, freight costs and price volatility at the Strait of Hormuz and Strait of Malacca, according to the IEA's ASEAN Energy Security Review published Oct. 6.
Oil demand is projected to grow by 130,000 b/d annually between 2025 and 2031, led by diesel, jet fuel, petrochemical feedstocks, LPG and marine fuels. Indonesia and Vietnam will account for much of the increase, according to the report.
Southeast Asia is expected to become the largest regional contributor to global oil-demand growth over the medium term, the IEA said. Regional consumption increased by an average of 1.7%/year in the decade through 2025, almost twice the global average.
LPG represents a particular vulnerability. Regional LPG imports have more than doubled over the past decade to around 500,000 b/d, while domestic natural gas liquids production is declining and alternative supply options remain limited, according to IEA.
Domestic supply declines Upstream production is moving in the opposite direction,
falling from 2.5 million b/d in 2015 to 1.8 million b/d in 2025. "Production is forecast to fall by 150 kb/d from 1.8 mb/d to 1.6 mb/d during 2025-2031, with Malaysia accounting for two-thirds of the drop," said the IEA.
However, the IEA highlighted Indonesia as an exception, with fiscal reforms, enhanced recovery and new condensate production expected to lift output following decades of decline. "Having fallen from approximately 820 kb/d in 2015 to 600 kb/d in 2025, output is projected to rise to around 730 kb/d by 2031, making Indonesia a notable exception among ASEAN producers," the agency said.
ASEAN has around 5.5 million b/d of refining capacity, with crude and condensate processing averaging 4.1 mb/d in 2026, according to IEA data. The region's refining sector continues to face competitive pressure from export-oriented refining centers in the Middle East and China and is expected to see limited further capacity additions through 2031, the IEA said.
Emergency oil stocks The IEA said emergency oil stocks remain the most effective
mechanism for managing a severe physical disruption because commercial inventories are generally intended for normal operations rather than prolonged international shortages.
ASEAN's Framework Agreement on petroleum security provides for consultation, information exchange and voluntary commercial assistance to members facing critical shortages, but practical implementation remains limited.
IEA recommended clarifying emergency procedures, improving regional market data, conducting simulation exercises and exploring commercially based regional storage arrangements

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

7

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 7, 2026

for smaller and highly import-dependent economies. It also said dedicated LPG stocks could merit separate consideration because the fuel requires distinct storage and distribution infrastructure.
The IEA noted that oil market balances are expected to flip from undersupplied in Q4 2026 to oversupplied by Q1 2027, with the surplus continuing throughout next year amid a rebound in Middle Eastern production and exports during 2027, providing governments an opportunity to "establish or expand governmentheld stocks of crude and products and absorb part of the surplus."
Biofuel demand to rise 50% The IEA expects Southeast Asian liquid biofuel demand to
increase approximately 50% from more than 360,000 b/d in 2025 to over 550,000 b/d by 2030. Biodiesel will account for 75% of that growth to 2030, with smaller contributions from ethanol, renewable diesel and sustainable aviation fuel.
Biofuel blending already displaces significant gasoline and diesel imports, including volumes equivalent to up to 35% of Indonesia's diesel demand, the IEA said.

Nigeria launches new 40-block oil and gas bid round
NUOPRC targets 1.74M b/d capacity boost
40 blocks span onshore, shallow, deep water
Previous round awarded 37 of 50 licenses
Nigeria has launched a fresh licensing round with 40 oil and gas blocks on offer, its state upstream regulator said Oct. 7, as Africa's largest producer pushes to grow its crude and condensate output to 3 million barrels/day by 2030.
The 2026 bid round comes just three months after the last one concluded in July. That saw Nigeria offer 50 licenses spanning the Niger Delta region, as well as the Benin, Anambra and Chad basins.
In all, 37 of the 50 licenses were awarded, and the concessions are expected to boost Nigeria's oil production by 300,000 b/d within three years, according to officials. Chevron was among the companies that secured new acreage in the process.
Oritsemeyiwa Eyesan, CEO of the Nigerian Upstream Petroleum Regulatory Commission (NUPRC), said the new auction is expected to add 1.74 million b/d to Nigeria's oil production capacity and 13.9 billion cubic feet/day to its gas capacity, once its fields are fully developed.
The licensing round was in keeping with Nigeria's target of growing its crude and condensate output to 3 million b/d of and gas production to 12 Bcf/d by 2030, she said.
"The round offers 40 blocks across land, shallow water and deep water terrains," she said, according to an NUPRC statement. "They are open to investors with the technical competence, the financial capacity and, above all, the commitment to develop

Nigeria's petroleum resources." Eyesan recently told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, in an
interview that the NUPRC plans to hold regular licensing rounds in a bid to resuscitate Nigeria's oil output, with 300,000-600,000 b/d of additional production targeted with each one.
"Our licensing will be regular and predictable," she said. "Our focus will be on what moves the numbers: restoring the more than 788,000 b/d of shut-in production identified across 63 operators; taking offshore projects valued at an estimated $30 billion to $50 billion to final investment decision; and raising domestic gas delivery from about two-thirds of the domestic obligation to full delivery."
Nigeria has previously struggled to meet its production targets, with maturing assets, theft and vandalism and underinvestment -- particularly in exploration -- eroding output in the past decade.
The OPEC member has recently pumped about 1.5 million b/d of crude, but output has not exceeded 2 million b/d for the past 12 years, according to the OPEC+ Survey from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
Nigeria's average oil production, including condensates, stood at 1.7 million b/d in August, according to government estimates.
In addition to the bid round, Eyesan said the NUPRC has begun to implement its "drill or drop" policy and will "actively score" and publish performance data on license holders.
The CEO said Sept. 30 that Nigeria's deepwater offshore gas and brownfield assets had been attracting renewed investment thanks to a combination of regulatory reforms, improved project economics and a growing pipeline of development-ready assets.
About 22 major offshore projects, comprising 12 deepwater and 10 shallow-water developments, have been identified for development, she said.
The projects include major developments such as Bonga Southwest, Aparo, Zabazaba, Owowo, Bosi and Egina South.
-- Newsdesk-Nigeria

Platts Asia Pacific Sweet Crude Daily Market Analysis
Two Dec-loading Ichthys Field condensate cargoes scheduled
Santos set to hold the sole Kutubu crude for the Dec loading cycle

The Asia-Pacific sweet crude market on Oct. 7 saw some crude and condensate released for the December-loading cycle, along with a Brunei Shell Petroleum sell tender offering Champion crude and Seria Light crude for loading in late December.
In the regional condensate complex, several trade sources noted that an initial loading program for Australia's Ichthys Field condensate had surfaced, with two 650,000-barrel cargoes scheduled for December loading.
A French company is set to hold the first cargo, loading over Dec. 11-15, while a Japanese company holds the other cargo scheduled to load over Dec. 22-26.
In the previous trading cycle, Inpex was heard to have sold the second Australian Ichthys Field condensate cargo for November, scheduled to load Nov. 16-20, to Glencore at a premium of around $16/b-$17/b to the Platts Dated Brent crude assessment, FOB, various crude traders said.
Prior to this, France's TotalEnergies was heard to have sold its Nov. 4-8 loading barrels of Ichthys Field condensate to South Korea's Hanwha Total at premiums ranging from $16/b to $20/b to Platts Dated Brent crude assessments, FOB, various crude traders said.
Meanwhile, on the light, sweet crude front, an Australian producer was heard to be holding the sole 650,000-barrel cargo of Papua New Guinea's light sweet Kutubu crude for loading over Dec. 25-29, multiple trade sources said.
In the previous cycle, oil company ExxonMobil, which holds the barrels for Papua New Guinea's light sweet Kutubu crude for loading over Nov. 27-Dec. 1, was heard to be reabsorbing the cargo back into the system, according to various market sources.
On the medium, sweet crude front, Brunei Shell Petroleum is offering a total of 600,000 barrels of Bruneian crude across two separate sell tenders, comprising a 300,000-barrel Champion Export cargo loading Dec. 25-26 and a 300,000-barrel Seria Light Export Blend cargo loading Dec. 27-28, according to a tender email seen by Platts, and sources with knowledge of the matter.

Platts Middle East Sour Crude Daily Market Analysis
Indian Oil buys Murban, Basrah Medium for Nov-Dec delivery
East-West oil pipeline flowing at 5.8 million b/d after restart: Saudi minister
Vitol CEO warns of shipping crisis as Middle East oil flows hit 14 million b/d
Activity picked up in the Middle East crude market Oct. 7 with Iraqi and UAE crude cargoes changing hands, while freight rates into Asia from across the globe firmed to fresh highs.

Indian Oil Corp. in a recently awarded tender bought 1 million barrels each of Murban crude and Basrah Medium crude for December delivery and mid-November delivery, respectively, in addition to a West African grade. The Murban parcel was heard awarded at a premium of around $13.65/b to Platts November Dated Brent on a DAP Vadinar basis, while the Basrah Medium was awarded at a premium of around $2/b to Dated Brent on a DAP Chennai basis.
Trade sources also noted a 2-million-barrel Basrah Medium cargo being placed with a Chinese independent refiner at a premium in the $20s/b to ICE Brent crude futures on a delivered basis, likely for delivery in November.
There was market talk that a Middle East producer was offering spot cargoes on an STS basis for November lifting at locations that included off West Coast India, though further details could not be ascertained.
An ongoing surge in freight rates has yet to plateau, with the cost of chartering a VLCC to Asia from across the globe firming further overnight and into the Oct. 7 session.
VLCC rates on the Gulf of Oman to Far East route hit a fresh high of $166.66/mt Oct. 7, up 1.8% day over day, while rates from West Africa and the US Gulf Coast to the Far East had also strengthened by 4%-6% overnight, Platts data showed.
The surging rates have likely prompted traders to break up their cargoes into smaller vessels, with a rare Aframax fixture for an early November-loading cargo from the US Gulf Coast to Asia seen being fixed, shipbroking reports showed Oct. 7.
Saudi Arabia's East-West pipeline returned to operations within "five or six days" after it was attacked and is pumping 5.8 million barrels/day of crude as of Oct. 6, according to Saudi energy minister Prince Abdulaziz bin Salman, Saudi news outlet Al Arabiya reported the same day.
Oil flows through the Middle East have reached as much as 14 million barrels/day over the past seven to 10 days, but the way in which transits through the Strait of Hormuz are conducted can intensify shipping bottlenecks and further increase freight costs, Vitol CEO Russell Hardy said Oct.6.
-- Yong ren Toh

Platts North Sea Crude Daily Market Analysis
High freight rates boost demand for short-haul grades
Traders expect improved November supply balances
Prompt North Sea values remained buoyed by demand for short-haul grades Oct. 7 as adjacent markets struggled with punitive freight rates, while prospects for further-out November loaders remained in flux.
As freight rates between Europe and key supply regions continued to reach record-high values, market participants noted interest for October-loading North Sea cargoes on comparatively lower cross-UK Continent freight rates for shorter journeys.
"The North Sea [market] is unique because of its short-haul nature, while the long-hauls are suffering," a Europe-based North Sea crude trader said.
"Freight is so high it breaks a lot of logistical logic," a second trader said, adding that some refiners had turned to shorter-haul North Sea grades "for security of supply."
The Platts Market on Close assessment process saw ExxonMobil bid for a Forties FOB Hound Point cargo loading across end-October to early November that was left outstanding at a $19/b premium over Dated Brent.
Traders had also pegged October-loading values for basket

grades, including Brent Blend and Forties, above the $20/barrel mark during European afternoon trading.
While Norway's Johan Sverdrup should see similar support in theory, with the grade having mostly cleared into Northwest Europe in recent cycles, ongoing pressure on differentials has been attributed by traders to cheaper alternative availabilities from the Middle East.
"Johan Sverdrup has suffered downward pressure as [Iraqi] Basrah Medium arrival offers were cheap, so the spread between heavy grades and lighter crudes such as the basket grades seems quite high," a third trader said.
Looking ahead, supply balances for November were expected to improve across both the sweet and sour segments, with producers across the Americas expected to prioritize Europe as an export destination to save on longer-haul freight costs to Asia.
As such, with fewer volumes headed East, market participants are anticipating more WTI Midland and Latin American crudes, which traditionally compete with Johan Sverdrup, headed to Europe, among others.
"For sure, Midland is coming," a fourth trader said. "The levels will depend on freight, as everything does these days, but shorter voyages would be prioritized."
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Natasha Tan
Platts West African Crude Daily Commentary
FOB differentials continue to decline amid thin spot deals
Sellers struggle to source bids from prospective buyers
Freight rates showed no sign of easing their bearish grip on the West African crude market Oct. 7 as sellers were said by market sources to be cutting offers with no bids in sight.
Indeed, elevated shipping economics had brought plenty of uncertainty into the WAF market, with spot trading activity limited as a consequence, one Europe-based WAF trader told Platts on the day.
"FOB differentials are collapsing but I don't think people in the market will move, for now," the trader added. "Some people are concerned about not finding vessels. I don't see cargoes moving and everything is a bit slower, it's hard to know what's going to happen."
Chad's Doba crude, one of the WAF region's more niche crude grades, was heard to be seeing offers for November stems at around a $10/barrel discount to Dated Brent mark on the day, with a second trader saying last-done levels were at premiums to the benchmark.
"Offers are just not representative of the market, people are lost and sellers are finding no bids," a third trader told Platts. "China are hinting that they need $25/b discounts for it to make sense [buying] Angolan grades."
Offers for Angolan crudes heard in the market on the day, including for grades such as Dalia and Girassol, were still holding above a $10/b discount to Dated Brent, market sources said.

Americas Crude
US crude oil inventories hit three-week low as exports surge
Commercial stocks fall 3.19 million barrels
SPR hits fresh 44-year low
Weekly crude exports climb 33% to 4-week high
US crude oil stocks fell to three-week lows during the week to Oct. 2, US Energy Information Administration data showed Oct. 7, against a backdrop of stronger exports and refinery demand.
US commercial crude stocks declined 3.19 million barrels over the week to Oct. 2 to 424.13 million barrels, US Energy Information Administration data showed Oct. 7. The draw was the largest since the week ended Aug. 28 and put stocks 0.6% below the seasonal five-year average, marking the first time they have fallen below normal since early August.
An additional 780,000 barrels exited from the Strategic Petroleum Reserves, extending declines for a 28th-straight week and putting stocks there down to 282.98 million barrels, a fresh 44-year low.
Commercial crude stocks declined across all regions outside of the Midwest, where planned and unplanned refinery outages have trimmed local demand. Midwest stocks climbed 1.87 million barrels, EIA data showed, including a 440,000-barrel build in inventories at the NYMEX delivery hub of Cushing, Oklahoma, that left stockpiles there at a 20-week high.
Midwest refinery utilization climbed 2.6 percentage points to 88.2% of capacity but remained well below the September average of around 94% of capacity. On Sept. 26, ExxonMobil restarted its 267,000 barrels per day Joliet refinery in Illinois after a power outage shut the plant unexpectedly on Sept. 13, according to a company spokesperson.

Nationwide refinery utilization edged 0.2 percentage point higher to 92.7% of capacity, pushing runs 5% above average for this time of year.
The bulk of the crude draw was seen on the US Gulf Coast, where crude stocks declined 3.9 million barrels as weekly exports surged 33% to a four-week high of 4.77 million barrels per day.
Exports have been supported by strong arbitrage economics. The incentive for moving USGC WTI MEH into Rotterdam has surged to $15.92/b versus North Sea Forties to-date in October, S&P Global Energy CERA data showed, up from $8.78/b in September and $1.41/b in August.
Nationwide gasoline stocks climbed 380,000 barrels to 204.74 million barrels, and distillate stocks dipped 40,000 barrels to 105.14 million barrels.
Distillate exports climbed 15% to a six-week high of 1.76 million b/d. These flows are likely to see pressure in the coming weeks

US imports of Canadian crude oil continued to climb in the week ended Oct. 2, according to data published by the US Energy Information Administration on Oct. 7.

Chevron divests Hess Midstream stake, restructures Bakken, DJ Basin assets
Eyes reducing midstream costs by 50%
Hess acquires midstream DJ basin assets
Chevron to write down $3.7 billion in Hess debt
Chevron Corp. said it would divest its ownership interests in US-based service provider Hess Midstream and restructure its midstream oil contracts in the Bakken and DJ Basin, it said.
The revised deal will extend Chevron's Bakken contracts and is expected to reduce its Bakken unit midstream costs by nearly 50%, enhancing future earnings and return on capital employed, Chevron said late Oct. 6.
Hess Midstream said in a separate statement that it would acquire Chevron's crude and natural gas gathering and storage assets in the DJ Basin, including a minority interest in a longhaul pipeline, diversifying its geographic presence and increasing revenue.
Chevron said the deal brought an "improved long-term

commercial framework," and $200 million cash consideration. Under the deal, Chevron expects to fully deconsolidate Hess
Midstream, including about $3.7 billion of Hess Midstream's debt, it said.
"This transaction resets the commercial framework between our upstream and midstream assets in the Bakken and DJ Basins," said Andy Walz, Chevron's president of downstream, midstream and chemicals, in a statement. "It lowers our Bakken cost structure while positioning Hess Midstream to advance as an independent company."
Chevron expects to sustain Bakken production through ongoing technology deployment and operational improvements drawn from its global shale and tight portfolio, it said.

Indonesia's sovereign wealth fund Danantara will invest $250 million in US oil and gas producer HighPeak Energy through convertible preferred stock, supporting development of its Permian Basin assets and technical exchanges to strengthen Indonesia's upstream capabilities, Danantara said Oct. 6.
The investment will be made through PT Danantara Investment Management. HighPeak will use the proceeds to strengthen its capital structure and financial flexibility and support further asset development, Danantara said in a statement.
HighPeak President and CEO Michael Hollis said the partnership would support continued investment, including an expanded drilling program.
Following completion of the transaction, HighPeak will develop training and secondment programs for Indonesians to build capabilities and exchange technical expertise, according to the statement.
Danantara said horizontal drilling and hydraulic fracturing, which have driven much of the Permian Basin's growth since 2007, could also improve productivity and recovery at mature onshore fields where reservoir conditions are suitable. These techniques offer a relevant model as Indonesia seeks to increase domestic oil and gas production, it said.
Under the investment terms, DIM will receive downside protection and an opportunity to benefit from future equity gains by converting its preferred stock into common shares. It will also have an option to subscribe to future HighPeak private placements and appoint one director to the company's board, Danantara said.
The completion is subject to applicable conditions and compliance with relevant policies, Danantara said.

Gulf of Mexico shut-ins seen at 511,619 boe/d, 350 MMcf/d due to tropical storm: MMA
25.08% of crude oil out impacted
Chevron to shut in at four facilities
Shell shuts in Mars production
Producers in the Gulf of Mexico have cumulatively shut in 511,619 barrels/day of oil equivalent crude oil and some 350 MM cubic feet/day of natural gas production in response to Tropical Storm Isaias, the Marine Minerals Administration said in its latest update Oct. 7.
MMA, which is part of the Bureau of Safety and Environment Assessment, said at 2 pm ET that the shut-in volumes represent about 25.08% of crude oil and 16.37% of natural gas production in the Gulf of Mexico.
"Based on data from offshore operator reports submitted as of 11 am CT today [Oct.7], personnel have been evacuated from a total of eight production platforms or 2.16% of the 371 manned platforms in the Gulf of America [Mexico]," MMA said, noting a single `dynamically positioned' rig has moved off location out of the storm's path as a precaution.
"Non-dynamically positioned rigs maintain their location while conducting well operations by using thrusters and propellers; these rigs are not moored to the seafloor, so they can move out of harm's way in a relatively short time frame," MMA said.
Chevron, Shell announce curtailment Chevron Corp. and Shell have started separately shutting in
production at their Gulf of Mexico facilities in preparation for Tropical Storm Isaias, they said Oct. 7, adding that all associated personnel are also being evacuated.
While some additional personnel are being transported onshore, production from four facilities have been shut in, while its other five Gulf of Mexico facilities remain at normal levels, Chevron said without identifying the offshore production units.
"At our onshore facilities, we continue to follow our established storm response procedures and are closely monitoring the projected path of the storm," it said.
"Shell is continuing to monitor Tropical Storm Isaias for potential impacts to our assets and operations in the Gulf of

America," it said, adding: "As a precautionary measure at our Mars, Olympus, Ursa, Vito, and Appomattox assets, we are evacuating all personnel and shutting in production."
In addition, Shell has already moved non-essential personnel from its Stones asset, it said.
Late Oct. 6, Chevron said it was keeping a close eye on the projected path of Tropical Depression Nine, while maintaining "normal" production levels from its assets in the Gulf of Mexico.
Chevron is a leading leaseholder in the Gulf of Mexico, operating nine facilities and multiple tie-backs, and has a goal of producing 300,000 boe/d net to the company in the current year, according to information on the Chevron website.
Tropical Depression Nine is a low-pressure system that is developing in the Gulf of Mexico, with a forecast to strengthen into Tropical Storm Isaias and subsequently a hurricane by Oct. 8, the National Hurricane Center said on its latest update on Oct. 7.
With a windspeed of 35 miles/hour, Tropical Depression Nine is located some 345 miles west of Progreso, Mexico, in the Bay of Campeche, the NHC said.
-- Ashok Dutta
South Korea may cut US crude costs with Panama pipeline FTA waiver
Transit time to drop by 15 days via new route
Country on track to keep Asia's top US crude buyer status
The Korea Customs Service is working to extend the preferential tariff treatment under the South Korea-US free trade agreement to US crude shipped through Panama's Petroterminal de Panamá pipeline, a move that could lower transportation costs and the time taken to move WTI Midland crude to East Asia via the Pacific Ocean passage, the customs agency and several industry sources with knowledge of the matter said during Oct. 2-7.
In an effort to reduce South Korea's dependence on Middle Eastern crude, the customs department has developed and implemented various measures to diversify import sources. For US crude imports, preferential tariffs under FTAs had previously been difficult to apply because the crude was shipped through neighboring countries after loading, potentially violating the "direct transportation" requirement for FTA preferential

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

14

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 7, 2026

treatment, the KCS said in a statement Oct. 5. South Korean refiners regularly purchase WTI Midland and
West Texas Light crude from the US Gulf Coast, and the sweet grades are often shipped directly to South Korea on VLCCs. On other voyages, refiners co-load US crude with West African, Middle Eastern or Southeast Asian grades, making several port calls along the long US Gulf Coast-Cape of Good Hope-Far East route, feedstock managers at two major refiners based in Ulsan and Seosan told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, during market discussion sessions over Oct. 5-7.
However, starting in May, the KCS said it eased the criteria for using FTAs, allowing such shipments to qualify for an exception to the direct transportation requirement when importers submit simplified supporting documents, such as a "cargo manifest and crude-oil type plan."
Thanks to the simplified FTA qualification structure, South Korea is poised to maintain its status as Asia's top US crude buyer in 2026, according to KCS and refinery feedstock managers. Imports from the US totaled 131.09 million barrels in JanuaryAugust, up 19.9% from the same period last year, the latest data from state-run Korea National Oil Corp. issued Sept. 25 showed.
KCS Commissioner Lee Jong-wook said in an Oct. 5 statement that the department will immediately begin working-level consultations with the Panamanian customs authorities so that, upon submission of certification documents issued by the Panama Customs Service or another competent authority, the shipments will be recognized as satisfying the FTA direct transportation requirement and "will qualify for preferential tariffs."
Should a pathway be established for US crude oil transported via the Panama Pipeline to receive FTA preferential tariff treatment, WTI Midland crude could be moved by pipeline from Panama's Atlantic Coast to the Pacific Coast and then reloaded onto a VLCC, the Ulsan-based refinery feedstock manager said.
US crude trading edge By seeking preferential FTA tariff treatment for US crude
arriving via the Panama pipeline's Pacific route, South Korea's customs authorities could give domestic refiners an additional trading advantage over other Asian refiners and trading firms, particularly as they already benefit from Seoul's freight incentives

for crude imported from outside the Middle East. Sources said strong demand from various Asian importers,
including South Korea, Japan, Taiwan and Thailand, has driven premiums for Atlantic Basin grades such as WTI Midland and Brazil's Tupi higher during recent trading cycles. Platts assessed WTI Midland on a DES Yeosu basis at a premium of $34.60/b to front-month Dubai on Sept. 18, marking a record-high price differential for the light, sweet US crude delivered to the Far East. The premium was last assessed at $28.50/b on Oct. 6.
Despite the high spot premium, the shorter Panama CanalPacific Ocean delivery option, on top of the government's freight incentives for non-Middle Eastern crude, would help cut costs, the refinery feedstock managers and a Singapore-based trader said. Compared to the US Gulf Coast-Cape of Good Hope-Far East route, VLCC transportation costs could be cut from $28.3 million to $24.3 million, according to KCS.
Additionally, compared with the existing Cape of Good Hope route, the transport time is expected to fall by approximately 15 days -- from 55 days to 40 days -- according to the Ulsan-based refinery feedstock manager and KCS.
The PTP Trans-isthmian Pipeline is 130 km long and it transports approximately 10 million barrels of crude per month from the Atlantic Terminal, located in Chiriquí Grande on the Atlantic Ocean, to the Pacific Terminal, located in Puerto Armuelles in the Pacific Ocean, the pipeline operator Petroterminal de Panamá S.A. said on its official website.
"Hundreds of oil tankers discharge and load over 100 million barrels of crude oil per year to take it to markets in Asia," Petroterminal de Panamá said.

Brazil's ANP sells 7 of 13 production-sharing blocks at 4th Open Acreage auction
Prio claims Magnetita, Hematita
CNOOC, Sinopec win Jade block
No competition for blocks sold
Brazil's National Petroleum Agency sold seven of the 13 production-sharing blocks available during the fourth cycle of the regulator's Open Acreage production-sharing bid round held Oct. 7, although the sale lacked competitive bidding, as each block was claimed with a single offer.
The auction raised $106.1 million in signing bonuses, with oil companies committing to an estimated $155.7 million worth of investments in seismic surveys and exploration wells, according to preliminary results.
The biggest surprise of the auction came from Brazilian independent oil and natural gas producer Prio, which claimed two production sharing blocks close to the company's Wahoo, Frade and Albacora Leste fields in the Campos Basin. Prio submitted the winning bids for the Magnetita and Hematita productionsharing blocks.
Prio agreed to an $18.7-million signing bonus and guaranteed Brazil a 32.8% share of profit oil for 100% of Magnetita, the ANP said. Prio also will pay a $316,000 bonus and guarantee 7.18% of

profit oil for 100% of Hematita. Magnetita and Hematita fit perfectly in Prio's portfolio, which
is concentrated in the northern section of the offshore Campos Basin. Magnetita surrounds Prio's Wahoo field, which pumped first oil in March as part of a tie-back project to the Valente floating production, storage and offloading vessel installed at the nearby Frade field. Magnetita also borders Frade and a portion of the Prio-operated Albacora Leste field, while Hematita is further north.
Prio's winning bids represented a strategic shift for the company, which has boosted output to near 200,000 barrels/ day of oil equivalent primarily through developing Wahoo and revitalizing mature fields already in production. Prio CEO Roberto Monteiro had indicated on the sidelines of the ROG.e oil and natural gas conference in late September that the company held little interest in greenfield exploration projects despite a stagnating market for mergers and acquisitions in Brazil.
"The company will evaluate the prospective resources in the block that, in case of success, could be developed by tie-back to the FPSO Valente located at the Frade field," Prio said in a regulatory filing after the auction. Prio expects to conduct a seismic survey and drill an exploration well in the blocks.
CNOOC, Sinopec team up again China National Offshore Oil Corp., or CNOOC, and China
Petroleum and Chemical Corp., or Sinopec, renewed a recent partnership to claim development rights to the Jade production sharing block in the Santos Basin, the ANP said. The two companies previously partnered to buy the Ametista block at the third Open Acreage production-sharing auction held in October 2025. CNOOC holds a 70% operating stake in Ametista, while Sinopec owns a 30% equity share.
The two companies agreed to a $20.9-million signing bonus and guaranteed Brazil an 18.2% share of profit oil to win development rights to Jade, which is east of the Libra productionsharing block containing the Mero field and south of the Alto do Cabo Frio Oeste and Alto do Cabo Frio Central productionsharing blocks, the ANP said. CNOOC will hold a 70% operating stake, while Sinopec will own a 30% equity share.
Brazilian state-led oil company Petrobras, meanwhile, claimed 100% operating stakes in the Azurita block in the Campos Basin and the Cruzeiro do Sul block in the Santos Basin, the ANP said.
Petrobras will pay a $19.7-million signing bonus and guarantee the government a 30% share of profit oil for Azurita, according to the ANP. The block is north of the Itaimbezinho production sharing block and the Equinor-operated Raia Manta and Raia Pintada fields, which are currently under development.
Equinor holds a 50% operating stake in Itaimbezinho, with Petrobras owning the remaining 50% stake. Equinor holds a 35% operating stake in the Raia project, with Petrobras retaining 30% and Spanish-Chinese joint venture Repsol Sinopec owning the remaining 35%.

Gasoline
US DATA: West Coast gasoline stocks fall alongside output and imports, EIA says
USWC gasoline inventories drop 134,000 barrels
Regional production falls 15,000 b/d on week
West Coast refinery utilization climbs to 95.2%
West Coast gasoline inventories decreased in the week ended Oct. 2, as lower regional production and a drop in imports offset higher overall refinery utilization, according to Energy Information Administration data released on Oct. 7.
Total USWC gasoline stocks fell 134,000 barrels on the week to 27.856 million barrels, reversing the prior week's increase. Regional stockpiles were 1.787 million barrels below levels recorded during the same period in 2025.
Foreign arrivals slowed across the Pacific Coast, contributing to the inventory draw. Gasoline imports into the region fell 21,000 b/d to average 52,000 b/d. On a year-over-year basis, incoming foreign shipments were 71,000 b/d lower.
Regional finished gasoline production also contracted, dropping 15,000 b/d to average 1.323 million b/d. Regional output trailed year-ago volumes by 110,000 b/d.
The drop in finished gasoline output contrasted with stronger top-line refinery activity. USWC refinery run rates rose 1.8 percentage points to 95.2%, standing 11.6 percentage points higher than the same week in 2025. The divergence highlights continued secondary unit constraints and distillate yield optimization across Pacific Coast plants even as throughput pushes past 95%.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed San Francisco regular CARBOB at NYMEX November RBOB futures plus 73 cents/gallon and assessed Los Angeles CARBOB at futures plus 69 cents/gal on Oct. 7.
-- Julia Martinez

US DATA: East Coast gasoline stocks surge despite refinery slowdown
Inventories climb 2.8 million barrels
Refinery utilization drops to 77.9%
Imports hold steady at 422,000 b/d
East Coast gasoline inventories posted a massive build during the week ended Oct. 2, as steady waterborne imports and seasonal post-summer demand easing outweighed a severe slump in regional refining activity, according to Energy Information Administration data released Oct. 7.
Total USAC gasoline stocks surged by 2.803 million barrels week over week to reach 53.743 million barrels. The sharp accumulation snapped multi-week lows and significantly narrowed the region's year-over-year inventory gap to 1.269 million barrels below 2025 levels.
The substantial stock build occurred despite a steep drop in Atlantic Coast refinery throughput. USAC refinery run rates fell 4.6 percentage points on the week to 77.9%, marking an acceleration in autumn turnaround activity. Utilization is now trailing year-ago levels by a stark 14.1 percentage points.
Tracking the lower crude throughput, finished gasoline output contracted by 36,000 b/d week over week to average 3.120 million b/d, sitting 128,000 b/d below the same period last year.
Meanwhile, foreign waterborne flows remained steady. Gasoline imports into the region edged up by 8,000 b/d week over week to average 422,000 b/d, maintaining the elevated pace set in late September. On an annual basis, import volumes were down by 61,000 b/d compared to the same week a year ago.
Platts assessed 13.5 RVP RBOB NYH barges at NYMEX November RBOB futures plus 10 cents/gallon on Oct. 7.
-- Julia Martinez

US gasoline markets were mixed Oct. 7 as Gulf Coast and Midwest inventories fell to historically low levels, supporting some values, while weaker premium-grade pricing in the Gulf Coast and broad declines in Chicago and West Coast differentials reflected ongoing regional supply and demand imbalances. Meanwhile, the Atlantic Coast saw pressure from a sharp inventory build and softer barge differentials, highlighting the divergence in regional market fundamentals despite generally tighter inventories elsewhere.
Gulf Coast US Gulf Coast gasoline differentials held steady as premium
CBOB and RBOB grades weakened Oct. 7, while regional gasoline and blending component inventories posted sharp withdrawals week over week.

USGC gasoline inventories posted a steep 1.83 million barrel withdrawal week over week, the EIA said. The draw follows an 804,000-barrel draw over the previous week, bringing regional inventories to their lowest level since September 2017.

Jet
Argentina's YPF-led venture signs deal to supply SAF to TotalEnergies
$400 million to be invested in the project
Sales deal is first step to finance the investment
Production will come from retrofitted oil refinery
YPF, Argentina's biggest oil refiner, said in a statement Oct. 7 that a consortium it leads signed a deal to supply sustainable aviation fuel to France's TotalEnergies under a long-term contract, a key for moving forward on the $400 million project at its San Lorenzo refinery.
State-backed YPF and Argentina-based Essential Energy will produce the SAF through their Santa Fe Bio venture at YPF's 50,000 b/d San Lorenzo refinery.
The first step to do this was inking a long-term sales contract, and the next steps will be to work on project engineering and to raise financing to retrofit the refinery to produce 170,000 mt/year of SAF and hydrotreated vegetable oil from animal fats, crop residues, vegetable oils and other related feedstock, YPF said.
The feedstock will be run through a biorefinery at the facility in the central farming province of Santa Fe, it added.
TotalEnergies will buy most of the output under a long-term contract with rights to the remaining volumes, YPF said.
YPF said last year that SAF demand is rising in Europe and the US, where airlines are seeking cleaner alternatives to jet fuel to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. The deal with TotalEnergies will allow it to meet this growing demand.
YPF acquired the 50,000 b/d San Lorenzo refinery in the central province of Santa Fe in 2018.
-- Charles Newbery

US exports of gasoline and jet fuel slipped in the week ended Oct. 2, while diesel exports continued to climb, according to data released Oct. 7 by the US Energy Information Administration.
Preliminary data indicated that an average of 831,000 barrels/day were exported for the most recent survey week, compared with 932,000 b/d for the week ended Sept. 25. The difference represented an average loss of 101,000 b/d, or about 10.8%.
Jet exports also declined to 327,000 b/d for the week ended Oct. 2, down from 358,000 b/d for the week ended Sept. 25, a decrease of 31,000 b/d, or about 9.5%.
Diesel exports increased, with 1.76 million b/d sent out for the week ended Oct. 2, up 235,000 b/d from the 1.53 million b/d exported the previous week, a 15.4% increase week over week. This was also the second-highest export figure for the second quarter of 2026 so far, behind the week ended Aug. 7, when almost 1.94 b/d were exported. Jet exports have continuously grown since the week ended Sept. 18, when exports were 1.33 million b/d.
US refined fuel exports to Latin America were expected to see a modest decrease for the week ending Oct. 10, with 2.07 million b/d across 52 shipments destined for the region, compared with 2.12 million b/d across 58 shipments for the week ended Oct. 3, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data.

Diesel
Transatlantic diesel arbitrage closes as US export ban prospects fade
Transatlantic diesel arbitrage turns negative
US exports to Europe drop 292,000 mt in Oct
European diesel refining margins plummet below jet
The transatlantic diesel arbitrage has closed as prospects faded for a US diesel export ban, which would have left Europe exposed to supply shocks.
On Oct. 2, the US Gulf Coast-Northwest Europe and USGCMediterranean diesel arbitrage incentives both entered negative territory for the first time since June 30, standing at minus $1.84/b and $3.71/b, respectively, Platts data showed. Since then, arbitrage incentives have weakened further, assessed at minus $3.09/b and minus $4.93/b on Oct. 6.
This comes largely with a sell-off in the front-month ICE low sulfur gasoil futures contract since the G7 announced an oil stock release and the ruling out of diesel export restrictions from the US.
On Oct. 6, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the October ICE low sulfur gasoil futures contract $53/mt lower day over day at $1,330.50/mt, the lowest level since Aug. 28.
"The transatlantic arb is now closed on a prompt basis, as the HOGO spread moved up quite significantly with slide on gasoil," said one Europe- based diesel trader.
"People were worried that the ban could happen at any minute," so "the decision has helped to ease the market somewhat," they added.
The downturn in gasoil futures Oct. 6 also coincided with reports that Russia was mulling a partial easing on its diesel export ban.
On Oct. 6, Interfax news agency reported that Russia could partially relax its diesel export ban by allowing some producers to export the product. This measure could be applied in October in order to prevent refineries from overstocking, according to the report.
This may have helped to soften market sentiment, with hopes of resupply.
"For sure it's good news," a Mediterranean diesel trader said.
Yet with falling European diesel prices and negative arbitrage incentives, Europe may now struggle to attract US barrels, which will tighten supply.
About 1.274 million mt of diesel/ gasoil is expected to be loaded in the US for Europe in October, down 292,000 mt from the month prior and 755,000 mt less than July, Kpler shipping data shows.
Additionally, weaker diesel refining margins in Europe will disincentivize diesel production in favor of its co-distillate jet, further reducing product availability.
The physical ARA diesel crack stood at $53.82/b on Oct. 6,

compared to $70.24/b for jet, implying a diesel-jet regrade of minus $16.43/b, Platts data showed.
"If refineries are not willing to refine diesel anymore as margins keep dropping... there will be some lack of product," said the Mediterranean diesel trader.
-- Sophia Aung
IEA supports priority release of diesel stocks 'to the extent possible'
IEA backs fast-tracked release of March commitment
Members have 200 million barrels of diesel still in stock
European release could leave 57 days of diesel supply: JP Morgan
The International Energy Agency has offered its support for the accelerated release of emergency oil reserves after a G7 commitment to speed up stock draws, but stopped short of setting specific timelines for new diesel and crude supplies to reach the market.
After a meeting of its member governments on Oct. 7, the IEA said it backed the faster release of stocks already agreed upon in March 2026, when the organization committed to draw an unprecedented 400 million barrels of crude and oil products from its collective reserves. Members also supported the prioritized release of diesel "to the extent possible, given the tightness in diesel markets," the statement said.
The IEA announcement follows an Oct. 2 statement from G7 leaders committing to release 100 million barrels of oil via the agency, after pressure from the US to take stronger measures to cool diesel prices. According to the IEA, 100 million barrels of oil represents the volume of stocks committed in March but not yet released, although some countries have overfilled their targets.
IEA member states -- comprising 32 mostly advanced economies -- have so far released 325 million barrels of oil in response to the Middle East supply shock, and the agency remains on standby to release more stocks as needed, according to its statement. Its remaining emergency oil stocks stand at 1.1 billion barrels, of which over 200 million are diesel.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

22

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 7, 2026

The IEA welcomed recent comments from the G7 on global energy security, and set out a target to complete remaining stock releases "as soon as possible". It did not repeat a G7 target for significant diesel releases to occur within the next 20 days, or for the full 100-million-barrel stock draw to be completed within the next four months.
Instead, the agency singled out the G7 commitment to "the importance of free flow of energy trade" after members jointly agreed to refrain from energy export restrictions. The commitment saw major importers like Europe stave off threats of a US diesel export ban, which US President Donald Trump had previously floated as a way to help curb domestic fuel costs.
"We're not going to be doing the export ban," Trump told reporters at the White House later the same day, acknowledging a European commitment to draw down more of its diesel stocks. "It was never really ever on the table, but what Europe did was a great thing," he said.
Emergency stocks policy The European Commission estimates that EU countries relied
on the US for half their diesel imports in August, leaving the bloc heavily exposed in the event of potential shut-ins. As prices continue to hover close to all-time highs, officials have warned of a "tough winter" ahead, but have maintained that supplies remain sufficient to serve the region's energy needs.
The new statement from the IEA appears to confirm analyst expectations that the G7's 100-million-barrel stock release commitment represents supplies already agreed in principle but slow to hit the market. According to analysis from JP Morgan, a US investment bank, Europe remains the largest pool of emergency diesel stocks, with 377 million barrels still in storage.
Major European contributors to the IEA's collective action include the Netherlands, France and Italy, although 40 million barrels of oil committed by the region in March have yet to reach the market. Assuming those volumes are made up exclusively using diesel stocks, the associated supply injection would be around 660,000 b/d, the bank estimates.
"A release of this size should materially ease prompt diesel tightness, narrow the transatlantic arbitrage and reduce Europe's reliance on US exports," said a note from JP Morgan analysts Natasha Kaneva, Lyuba Savinova, and Artem Fakhretdinov.
However, governments are likely to remain anxious around deploying reserves that could help shield them through any further deterioration in energy supplies, the analysts said, estimating a 40-million-barrel diesel release would leave Europe with around 57 days of demand cover.
The IEA's governing board is set to meet again next week after continued assessment of the market situation, the statement said, without providing further detail.
-- Kelly Norways

Eastern USGC refiners brace for Isaias, as the storm threatens diesel exports
Refiners brace for Isaias' operational impact
Storm threatens 2.5 mil b/d of refining capacity
USGC exports more than 90% of distillate produced
Refineries along the eastern edge of the US Gulf Coast have braced for the possible impact of Tropical Storm Isaias, which finally emerged after an unusually quiet Atlantic hurricane season, with the National Hurricane Center forecasting rapid strengthening and several forecasters expecting it to become the first Atlantic hurricane of 2026.
"At our onshore facilities, we are following our storm preparedness procedures and paying close attention to the forecast and track of the system," a spokesperson with Chevron Corp. said in a statement, noting that it is likely that the company will continue to monitor and likely provide operational updates for the storm, which is "pretty dynamic."
Under the storm's current path, about 2.5 million barrels/ day, or about 14% of total US refining capacity, is at risk. Multiple models forecast that Isaias could strengthen to a Category 2 storm before coming ashore later this week, making it the first hurricane of the 2026 Atlantic Season, according to National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA) data.
Meteorologists note that if Isaias reaches hurricane status Oct. 8, it would tie the record, set in 1905, for the latest first Atlantic hurricane. If it happens after Oct. 8, it would be a new record.
As the storm approaches the northern Gulf Coast, US Gulf Coast traders will be closely monitoring forecast track shifts, refinery operating decisions, Coast Guard port conditions, ship traffic restrictions, power-reliability risks, and the potential for flooding-related impacts on refineries, terminals, and logistics.
As of 4 am CT, NHC data showed the storm about 580 miles southwest of the Mississippi River, but it is forecast to strengthen rapidly, potentially reaching hurricane intensity Thursday, pass north of the Yucatan Peninsula, and approach the northern USGC Oct. 9.
The Coast Guard's Sector New Orleans has set Port Condition Whiskey at 7 am CT, Oct. 7, in preparation for Tropical Storm Isaias. Under Port Condition Whiskey, gale-force winds are possible within 72 hours. No coastal watches or warnings are in effect, but a hurricane watch could be issued later Oct. 7.
The port of Mobile, near refineries in Alabama and Mississippi, is already under Port Condition Whiskey.
Diesel export impact Given the role USGC refiners have played in supplying tight
global diesel markets, any storm-related outage could have supply repercussions in Latin America and Europe. Between July 2025 and June 2026, more than 95% of the distillate that USGC refiners produced, primarily ultra low sulfur diesel (ULSD), was exported.

Over the past 12 months, the USGC has exported 1.18 million barrels/day of diesel and gasoil, according to data from S&P Global Commodities at Sea, with most going to Latin America.
For the past 12 weeks, this has risen to 1.45 million b/d, with the UK moving up in the ranks of top-10 export destinations, importing 103.1 million b/d, up from 67.3 million b/d imported over the last 12 months, when it was in 10th place.
Of the 1.45 million b/d of exports seen over the past 12 weeks, 30% came from the key eastern US Gulf Coast refineries, where volumes have risen.
Exports of diesel and gasoline from key eastern USGC refineries in Alabama, Mississippi, and eastern Louisiana have averaged 434,080 b/d over the past 12 weeks, according to CAS. This is almost 18% higher than the 12-month average of 368,736 b/d.
In Pascagoula, Mississippi, where Chevron has a 356,440 b/d refinery, diesel exports rose 39%, from 56,870 b/d over the past 12 months to 79,010 b/d over the past 12 weeks.
According to the latest Energy Information Administration Weekly Petroleum Status Report for the week ended Sept. 25, US distillate fuel oil exports, largely comprising ULSD and other diesel-grade barrels from the USGC, averaged 1.529 million b/d, up 198,000 b/d or 15%, from 1.331 million b/d the previous week.
The Platts USGC ULSD prompt pipeline crack averaged $89.29/barrel Oct. 6, climbing just shy of 1% from Oct. 5 but below a record $105.42/b Sept. 15.
ULSD inventories slip EIA weekly data shows for the week ended Sept. 25, USGC
refiners continued to run at very high rates despite a modest week-over-week decline to 95.9% from 96.5%, while crude oil inputs fell 297,000 b/d to 9.20 million b/d.
Even with the decline, Gulf Coast refinery runs remained well above year-ago levels, reflecting strong refining economics and robust operating rates heading into maintenance season.
Meanwhile, PADD 3 ULSD inventories dipped 139,000 barrels week over week to 35.87 million barrels, following several weeks of stock builds, thus pulling down total USGC distillate inventories by 504,000 barrels to 43.90 million barrels for the week ended Sept. 25, according to EIA data.

US DATA: Diesel exports hit a six-week high, pulling stocks down, EIA says
West Coast stocks fall to 2012 lows
Exports rise to six-week highs
Nationwide production crosses 5 million b/d
A surge in US ultra low sulfur diesel (ULSD) exports pulled nationwide inventories to a six-week low and sent West Coast stocks to their lowest level since 2012, according to the latest US Energy Information Administration data released Oct. 7 for the week ended Oct. 2.

Nationwide ULSD inventories fell 655,000 barrels to 93.73 million barrels, a six-week low. The largest draw came from the Gulf Coast, which shed 481,000 barrels to end the week at 35.39 million barrels, its lowest level since the week ended Sept. 4. The West Coast followed, with a 469,000-barrel draw bringing inventories to 8.9 million barrels, the lowest level for the region since 2012.
The draw on stocks was primarily driven by a significant increase in exports, which rose 235,000 barrels/day to 1.76 million b/d, the highest since the week ended Aug. 21. The pull on barrels for export was likely supported by a wide-open arbitrage incentive to Europe, which averaged $4.05/barrel during the same EIA reporting period.
In contrast to the inventory draws, nationwide diesel production increased 224,000 b/d to 5.066 million b/d, crossing the symbolic 5 million b/d threshold and marking the highest level in four weeks. Most of the production increase came from the Midwest, with output rising 173,000 b/d to 1.247 million b/d. The Midwest was the only region to see an inventory build, adding 207,000 barrels to end the week at 25.34 million barrels. The main refining hub in the US, the Gulf Coast, also increased production by 50,000 b/d to 2.92 million b/d, its highest since the week ended May 29.
Platts assessed US Gulf Coast ULSD outright prices lower on Oct. 6, falling by 1.43 cents/gallon to close at $4.5344/gal, as the Trump administration announced an executive order to temporarily expand access to tax-exempt red-dye diesel fuel to mitigate rising prices across the US.
Product supplied, a proxy for demand, fell 298,000 b/d to 3.65 million b/d, a three-week low. Despite a dip in domestic demand and a rise in production, which would typically support inventory builds, nationwide stocks failed to recover due to a substantial week-over-week increase in exports.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Carlos Castillo
INDIA DATA: Middle distillates demand rises 5% YOY to 7.93 mil mt in Sep
Gasoil demand rises 5.4% YOY
Aviation turbine fuel demand inches up 1.5% YOY
India's middle distillate demand rose 1.8% month over month and 5% year over year to 7.93 million metric tons in September, provisional data from the Petroleum Planning and Analysis Cell showed Oct. 7.
India consumed 7.16 million mt (1.78 million barrels/day) of gasoil in September, up 2% from the 11-month low of 7.02 million mt in August, the data showed. Gasoil demand increased 5.4% from 6.79 million mt in September 2025, according to the data.
The country's cumulative gasoil demand over JanuarySeptember rose 5% year over year to 72.32 million mt, the data showed.
